A practical path: Improve previous program by:
1. Use better initial sequence from solving an easier 1D continuous optimization. Perhaps we can run a low-dimensional optimization (e.g., 128 or 512 length) to find a profile and then upsample to large n via some rule.

Potential new idea: Use "alternating minimization / quantum"? Or "mirror descent on log variables" because a_i decay like power law; optimizing in log domain may handle scales. We can parameterize a_i = exp(x_i - logsumexp(x)) (softmax) with x length n. Sum=1. Objective M=sum? Then M=max conv(softmax, softmax). Gradient w.r.t x: d a/dx = a_i (δ - 1/S? M derivative g_i=∂M/∂a_i. dM=∑_i g_i a_i(δ_{ij}-a_j)=a_j( g_j - ∑_k g_k a_k). So gradient in x is a_j (g_j - dot(g,a)). This keeps sum=1 automatically. We can use mirror descent: x -= step * a_j(g_j - dot(g,a)). This is exactly multiplicative updates (mirror descent) with KL Bregman. Let's develop an algorithm:
```
def log_mirror_step(a, beta, eta, rng):
   c = conv(a,a)
   # grad_logM
   g = lse_log_gradient(a,c,beta) # returns grad_logLSE
   mu = a*g
   mu_sum = mu.sum() # should 2, maybe due numeric
   d = mu - 2.0/len(mu)
   d -= d.mean()
   # clip d
   d = np.clip(d, -max_log, max_log)
   a_new = a * np.exp(-eta*d)
   a_new = normalize(a_new)
   return a_new, c, score
```

`convolve_self(a)`:
```
n=len(a); size=2*n-1; fsize=1 << (size-1).bit_length(); A=fft(a, fsize); c=np.real(ifft(A*A, fsize))[:size]; return np.maximum(c,0)
```

`gradient_from_weights(a, weights, A_fft, fsize)`:
```
W=fft(weights, fsize)
g=2.0*np.real(ifft(W*np.conj(A_fft), fsize))[:len(a)]
return g
```

Implement `run_log_mirror`:
```
def run_log_mirror(a_init, max_time, rng, beta=50.0, eta=0.25, lambda_refine_every=50, lambda_iters=10, max_active=4096, perturb_every=2000, ...):
    a=normalize(a_init,1.0)
    best_a=a; best_score=score(a)
    start=...
    it=0; last_improve=0; eta_current=eta
    vel=np.zeros(n) # momentum maybe
    while time < max_time:
       it+=1
       c=conv(a); score=2n*c.max()
       if score<best_score: update
       # choose weights
       if need_refine and it%lambda_refine_every==0:
           active_idx = lags where c >= cmax - tol; cap top max_active
           lam = refine_lambda(a, active_idx, lambda_iters, ...)
       else:
           weights = lse_weights(c,beta)
       grad=gradient_from_weights(...)
       mu=a*grad; normalize; d=mu -2/n; center/clip
       # if residual small and no improvement, perturb
       # momentum? maybe vel = 0.8*vel +0.2*d, but with nonconvex. Let's use simple `step_vec = d` initially, optional momentum when stable.
       step_vec = d
       # adaptive step: base_eta * min(1, 0.5/std?)? Try:
       rms = sqrt(mean(d*d))
       if rms < 1e-12: no change
       step = eta_current / max(1.0, 20.0*rms) ? If rms=0.01, 20*0.01=0.2 =>step large; if rms=0.5, denom10=>0.05.
       # cap [0.01, 1.0]
       step = min(1.0, max(0.02, eta_current * (0.2/rms)))? uncertain.
       a_try = a * exp(-step*step_vec); normalize
       # if step too large maybe objective worsens. We can line search a few steps: for factor [1, 0.5, 0.25]
       accepted=False
       for factor in [1.0,0.5,0.25,0.1]:
           a_try = normalize(a * exp(-(step*factor)*step_vec))
           s_try = score(a_try)
           if s_try < score * 1.0001:
               a=a_try; accepted=True; break
       if not accepted:
           # try tiny step or perturb
           a=normalize(a*(1 - 1e-4*step_vec))
       if not improve for many: perturb; reduce eta
       reset momentum if accepted? 
    return best_a,best_score
```
Line search each iter with up to 3 convs expensive. We can reduce: use a single step with line search only if score worse than previous current after update. Maybe:
- compute a_new with step; score_new once. If score_new < score_current, accept; else multiply step by 0.5 and try up to 3 times. So usually 1-2 convs. But we already computed c for current; after update need new score. That's one extra conv per iter. Acceptable.

Could integrate: compute current c at start; after accept, next loop computes c. The line search conv can be skipped by just using current c? No, a changed; need score. Unless we do "trust region" and only score every few iterations. For local final, score expensive but okay. For n=32768, score 65k FFT ~0.005s. 80k iters =>400s just scoring plus grad another 400s, okay.

Use `score(a)` uses conv; same as c? We can compute c at start and use score; to evaluate a_try, need new conv. Could optimize by using same FFT for a_try if small? no.

Maybe for run_log_mirror, use estimated improvement from d: directional derivative = -∑ d_i^2? Since log-mirror approximate; but to be safe score.

Now `refine_lambda` implementation.

```
def refine_lambda(a, active_idx, max_iters=10, lr_init=0.1, fsize=None, A_fft=None):
```

Let's write `refine_lambda` with multiplicative λ update:
```
def refine_lambda(a, active_idx, A, fsize, n_iters=8, step=0.5):
    n=len(a); L=2*n-1
    lam=np.zeros(L)
    lam[active_idx]=1.0/len(active_idx)
    lam_active=lam[active_idx].copy() # projection mask
    for it in range(n_iters):
       W=fft(lam,fsize)
       mu=2*real(ifft(W*conj(A)))[:n]
       s=mu.sum(); if s<1e-300: break
       mu *= 2/s
       d=mu - 2.0/n
       # variance maybe
       D=fft(d,fsize)
       v=real(ifft(D*A,fsize))[:L]
       # grad for active
       g=v[active_idx]
       # center g to account for sum constraint: only differences matter
       g = g - np.mean(g)
       # adaptive lr: if g has outliers, scale
       gmax = max(np.max(np.abs(g)),1e-12)
       lr = step / gmax
       # ensure lam positive for multiplicative
       la = np.maximum(lam_active, 1e-14)
       la *= np.exp(np.clip(-lr*g, -10,10))
       la /= la.sum()
       lam_active=la
       # assign to lam active? We can keep only active vector; for FFT, construct full lam each iter:
       lam[active_idx]=la
    return lam
```
But we also need initial lam active; uniform. The multiplicative update with centering g: if g positive, decrease λ; negative increase. `lr/gmax` gives step ~1 if max g. With K large, sum changes; normalization. Might converge.

Potential issue: active_idx as boolean of length L; `v[active_idx]` cost. If active large, okay. If active_idx list, use `lam[active_idx]=la`. Need active_idx as int array to subtract mean. We'll generate int array sorted.

```
def run_basin_hopping_log(seed_seq, n, S, max_time, rng, local_time=45):
   a=normalize(seed_seq,S)
   best_a,best_score=run_log_mirror(a, local_time, rng,...)
   while time left:
      # mutate best or random from population
      if rng.random()<0.7: base=best_a else base=random init/resample
      mut=fourier_phase_mutate(base,rng,scale=0.1)
      a_opt,score=run_log_mirror(mut, local_time, rng,...)
      if score<best: update
   return best
```

```
def central_binomial_seq(n, rng=None):
    a=np.ones(n)
    for i in range(1,n): a[i]=a[i-1]*(2*i-1)/(2*i)
    return a
```

Resampling function:
```
def resample_positive(src, N):
    src=np.asarray(src,float); src=np.clip(src, 1e-15, 1e15)
    m=len(src)
    if m==N: out=src.copy()
    else:
       xold=np.linspace(0,1,m)
       xnew=np.linspace(0,1,N)
       yold=np.log(src)
       ynew=np.interp(xnew, xold, yold)
       out=np.exp(ynew)
    s=out.sum(); if s<... out=ones; return out/s
```
This preserves shape.

`choose_lambda` implementation:
```
def choose_lambda(c, a, A, fsize, beta, max_active, refine_iters):
    M = c.max()
    # active tolerance
    tol = 1e-5 * M + 1e-300
    cands = np.flatnonzero(c >= M - tol)
    if len(cands) == 0: cands = np.array([np.argmax(c)])
    if len(cands) > max_active:
        # choose top max_active by c
        part = np.argpartition(c, -max_active)[-max_active:]
        cands = part[np.argsort(c[part])[::-1]]
    if refine_iters > 0 and len(cands) > 1:
        lam = refine_lambda(a, cands, A, fsize, refine_iters)
        if lam is not None:
            return lam
    # fallback LSE
    w = lse_weights(c, beta)
    return w
```
But `np.flatnonzero` over c every refine expensive but okay. Could simply top max_active by argpartition, no need tolerance; if active lags just below max but important, top max includes. For KKT maybe need all; but max_active=8192 out of 65535 maybe enough? If optimum requires all lags uniformly, top8192 suboptimal. LSE fallback with beta high might include all near top; but if we cap top, may bias. Could use tolerance to include all if count ≤max_active; else if count > max_active use top max_active. For flat sequences with many near max, count >max; top might not represent average. In that case, LSE with moderate beta might be better. We can if count > max_active, use LSE instead of refine on top? But refine on top maybe better to target peaks. For final polish, active peaks dominate. We'll implement: if count <= max_active, refine on all active; else use LSE with beta (which also weights all by exponential, mostly top). Or refine on top plus LSE? Let's do:
- count active within tol.
- if count <= max_active and count >= 2: refine on active.

Now `fourier_phase_mutate` for basin hopping. We can use `project_simplex` to sum S=1. For log-mirror sequences with zeros? floor. Function:
```
def fourier_phase_mutate(a, rng, scale=0.1):
   n=len(a); S=a.sum()
   fsize=1<<(2*n-1).bit_length()
   A=fft(a,fsize); mag=np.abs(A); phase=np.angle(A)
   # noise scale frequency-dependent
   freqs=np.arange(fsize)
   weight=1.0+0.2*freqs/fsize
   noise=rng.normal(0, scale*weight, fsize)
   new_A=mag*np.exp(1j*(phase+noise))
   new_A[0]=A[0]
   out=np.real(ifft(new_A,fsize))[:n]
   return project_simplex(out, S)
```
Can make mutation smaller in final. Basin hopping local time short.

```
def run_basin_hopping_log(a_init, max_time, rng, local_time, max_active, lambda_iters, beta, step_initial):
   a=normalize(a_init,1.0)
   best_a,best_score=run_log_mirror(a, local_time, rng, ...)
   start=time.monotonic()
   population=[(best_score,best_a)]
   best_a2=best_a
   while time.monotonic()-start < max_time - local_time:
       progress=...
       scale=0.2*(1+0.5*progress?)
       if len(population)>1 and rng.random()<0.3: base=population[rng.integers(len)][1]
       else: base=best_a
       mut=fourier_phase_mutate(base,rng,scale)
       a_opt,s=run_log_mirror(mut, local_time, rng, ...)
       if s<best_score: update
       # also if s < best in population, add
       population.append((s,a_opt.copy()))
       if len(population)>6: sort by score and keep 6
   return best_a, best_score
```

`normalize_positive`:
```
def normalize_positive(a, S=1.0):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    a=np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    a=np.clip(a,0.0,1.0/S if S>0 else 0,?) # cap 1000? For sum S, cap 1000.
    a=np.maximum(a,0.0)
    s=a.sum()
    if s <= 1e-15:
       return np.full(len(a), S/len(a))
    a = a*(S/s)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    # after clipping sum may change; renormalize maybe
    s2=a.sum()
    if s2 <=1e-15: return ...
    a = a*(S/s2)
    return a
```
Don't cap too low before sum. Values ≤1 if S=1. Use clip 1000.

`fft_size(n)` top-level:
```
def fft_size(n):
    size=2*n-1
    return 1 << (size-1).bit_length()
```

`lse_weights`:
```
def lse_weights(c,beta):
    cmax=np.max(c)
    q=beta*(c-cmax)
    e=np.exp(q)
    s=e.sum()
    if s<=0: w=np.zeros_like(c); w[np.argmax(c)]=1; return w
    return e/s
```
Since q max 0, e[ cmax ]=1. Good.

`gradient_from_weights` maybe inline.

`refine_lambda` details. Need be careful with orientation for v. We want dμ_i/dλ_s = 2 a_{s-i} (like gradient). For λ update to reduce Var(μ), derivative wrt λ_s = 2∑_i d_i a_{s-i}. This is exactly `?` Our `v` from `ifft(D*A)` gives coefficient v_k =∑_i d_i a_{?}. Let's compute: D_k=∑_i d_i e^{-2πi i k/N}; A_k=∑_j a_j e^{-2πi j k/N}; ifft D*A at index s: ∑_{i,j} d_i a_j δ_{s-i-j≡0}=∑_i d_i a_{s-i}. Yes v_s. Good. dμ/dλ_s = 2 a_{s-i}? Wait μ_i = 2∑_t λ_t a_{t-i}. Derivative wrt λ_s =2a_{s-i}. Sum over i gives 2∑_i d_i a_{s-i}=2 v_s. So grad_var = (4/n? no) 4? Let's derive Var =∑(μ_i-mean)^2, mean=2/n. If λ sum1, mean constant? μ_i scale fixed sum2, mean=2/n constant. dVar =2∑d_i dμ_i =2∑d_i 2a_{s-i}=4v_s. So gradient =4v. We can use v (scale arbitrary). Multiplicative update λ *= exp(-lr*v_centered). Sign: if v>0, increasing λ increases variance? Yes dVar/dλ=4v positive, so decrease λ: exp(-lr*v). Good.

But μ after `mu *= 2/sum` introduces scaling; if sum changes with λ, derivative includes scaling. However if λ sum1, for any weights, sum μ =2∑w c_s, not necessarily constant 2 unless c_s equal. Refinement wants flat μ after normalization; scaling matters. We can ignore; heuristic okay.

Maybe better to minimize Var(μ/sum) (normalized). Derivative more complex. But heuristic likely okay.

`refine_lambda` active vector: If `active_idx` int array length K.
```
def refine_lambda(a, active_idx, A, fsize, max_iters=8, step=0.5):
    n=len(a); L=2*n-1
    K=len(active_idx)
    if K==0: return None
    lam=np.zeros(L)
    if K==1:
       lam[active_idx[0]]=1.0
       return lam
    lam[active_idx]=1.0/K
    for it in range(max_iters):
       W=fft(lam,fsize)
       mu=2.0*np.real(ifft(W*np.conj(A), fsize))[:n]
       smu=mu.sum()
       if smu<1e-300: return lam
       mu *= 2.0/smu
       d = mu - (2.0/n)
       D=fft(d,fsize)
       v=np.real(ifft(D*A,fsize))[:L]
       g=v[active_idx]
       g = g - np.mean(g)
       gmax=np.max(np.abs(g))
       if gmax < 1e-14:
           break
       lr = step / gmax
       la = np.maximum(lam[active_idx], 1e-14)
       la *= np.exp(np.clip(-lr*g, -10, 10))
       la /= la.sum()
       lam[active_idx] = la
    return lam
```
If active_idx not sorted, no issue.

`choose_lambda`:
```
def choose_lambda(c, a, A, fsize, beta, max_active, refine_iters):
    L=2*len(a)-1
    cmax=c.max()
    # tolerance maybe 1e-5 cmax
    tol=1e-5*cmax
    active = np.flatnonzero(c >= cmax - tol)
    if len(active) == 0:
       active=np.array([np.argmax(c)])
    if len(active) > max_active:
       part=np.argpartition(c, -max_active)[-max_active:]
       # sort by c descending
       part=part[np.argsort(c[part])[::-1]]
       active=part
    if refine_iters > 0 and len(active) >= 2:
       return refine_lambda(a, active, A, fsize, refine_iters)
    return lse_weights(c,beta)
```
If active count large > max, refine on top max; okay. `argpartition` with kth=-max_active; if max_active >= L, avoid. Ensure max_active < L.

In run, after choose_lambda returns lam, need `W=fft(lam,fsize)`. If lam from lse_weights maybe full length.

`run_log_mirror` pseudo code in more detail:
```
def run_log_mirror(a_init, max_time, rng, beta=50.0, step_initial=0.2, max_active=4096, lambda_iters=8, refine_freq=64, max_step=1.0, min_step=0.01):
    a=normalize_positive(a_init,1.0)
    n=len(a); fsize=fft_size(n); size=2*n-1; two_n=2.0*n
    # quick initial score
    A=fft(a,fsize); c=np.real(ifft(A*A,fsize))[:size]; np.maximum(c,c,out=c)
    best_score=two_n*c.max(); best_a=a.copy(); prev_score=best_score
    start=time.monotonic(); it=0; last_improve=0
    step=step_initial
    stale_threshold=2000
    beta_current=beta
    max_active_local = min(max_active, size)
    # choose initial lam
    lam=choose_lambda(c,a,A,fsize,beta_current,max_active_local,lambda_iters)
    W=fft(lam,fsize)
    while time.monotonic() - start < max_time:
       it += 1
       # If periodic: choose new lam? We compute A,c for current a each iter.
       A=fft(a,fsize)
       temp=ifft(A*A,fsize); c=temp.real[:size]; temp.real? can't modify? Use np.real(ifft(...))
       np.maximum(c,c,out=c)
       score=two_n*c.max()
       if score < best_score - 1e-15:
           best_score=score; best_a=a.copy(); last_improve=it
       # adapt step score changes
       if score < prev_score - 1e-15:
           step = min(max_step, step*1.02)
       elif score > prev_score + max(1e-8, 1e-6*prev_score):
           step = max(min_step, step*0.85)
           # if too far from best, revert
           if score > best_score * 1.05:
               a = best_a.copy()
               A=fft(a,fsize); c=...; score=...? We can just continue next loop? set a, prev_score=score? Simpler fallthrough: after revert, recompute? To save, set a=best_a, step*=0.5, W maybe old. We can continue without recomputing? Next loop recompute. But current score bad for gradient; skip update this iter: continue.
       prev_score = score
       # if stale, perturb
       if it - last_improve > stale_threshold:
           noise = 0.04 * np.exp(-0.1 * (it-last_improve)/stale_threshold)
           a = normalize_positive(a * np.exp(rng.normal(0, noise, n)),1.0)
           step = max(min_step, step*0.5)
           beta_current *= 1.1
           last_improve = it  # temporary? Maybe not to avoid repeat perturb; set a flag? If set, loop may not improve; okay.
           continue
       # update lambda periodically
       if it % refine_freq == 0:
           max_active_dyn = min(max_active_local, size)
           lam = choose_lambda(c, a, A, fsize, beta_current, max_active_dyn, lambda_iters)
           if lam is not None:
               W = fft(lam, fsize)
           # update beta
           beta_current = min(5000.0, beta_current*1.03)
       # gradient
       g = 2.0*np.real(ifft(W*np.conj(A), fsize))[:n]
       mu = a * g
       smu = mu.sum()
       if smu <= 1e-300:
           perturb and continue
       mu *= 2.0 / smu
       d = mu - (2.0 / n)
       d -= d.mean()
       # clip
       maxd = np.max(np.abs(d))
       if maxd < 1e-12:
           # near KKT; maybe perturb small if stagnant
           if it - last_improve > stale_threshold//2:
               a = normalize_positive(a * np.exp(rng.normal(0,0.02,n)),1.0)
           continue
       d = np.clip(d, -5.0, 5.0)
       # update
       a_try = a * np.exp(-step*d)
       a = normalize_positive(a_try,1.0)
    return best_a, best_score
```
The `continue` after perturb makes current iter not update; okay. But last_improve resetting to it on perturb may prevent repeated perturb; but if no improvement, it can go up to 2000 then perturb, then another 2000. Good. If maxd small and not stale, continue without updating; could waste time. We can instead perturb small once: if maxd small and it-last>500, perturb.


`run_basin_hopping_log` should avoid nested functions, no lambdas. It calls run_log_mirror. Sorting population by tuple. Need pass parameters. Simpler basin:
```
def run_basin_hopping_log(a_init, max_time, rng, local_time, beta, step_initial, max_active, lambda_iters):
    n=len(a_init)
    if local_time <=0: return a_init, np.inf
    best_a,best_score=run_log_mirror(a_init, local_time, rng, beta=beta, step_initial=step_initial, max_active=max_active, lambda_iters=lambda_iters)
    start=time.monotonic()
    population=[(best_score, best_a.copy())]
    while time.monotonic()-start < max_time - local_time:
       progress=(time.monotonic()-start)/max(1.0,max_time)
       scale = 0.15 * (1.0 + 0.5*(1.0-progress))
       r=rng.random()
       if len(population)>1 and r < 0.3:
          base = population[rng.integers(len(population))][1]
       else:
          base = best_a
       mut = fourier_phase_mutate(base, rng, scale)
       a_opt,score=run_log_mirror(mut, local_time, rng, beta=beta, step_initial=min(step_initial,0.2)?)
       if score < best_score:
          best_score=score; best_a=a_opt.copy()
       population.append((score, a_opt.copy()))
       if len(population)>6:
          population.sort()
          population=population[:6]
    return best_a,best_score
```
`rng.integers` returns numpy int; use as index. No lambda. `r<0.3` with float.

Time in while: if local_time close to max_time, no hops. Ensure `local_time` not too large. For short calls, local_time maybe 40, max_time 200.

```
def generate_inits(n, rng, given=None, short_best=None):
    inits=[]
    if given is not None and len(given)>0:
       inits.append(normalize_positive(given,1.0).copy())
    if short_best is not None:
       inits.append(normalize_positive(short_best,1.0).copy())
    c=central_binomial_seq(n)
    inits.append(normalize_positive(c,1.0))
    inits.append(normalize_positive(c[::-1],1.0))
    # mixture of central and reversed? Maybe not.
    if given is not None and len(given)!=n:
       inits.append(resample_positive(given,n))
    if short_best is not None and len(short_best)!=n:
       inits.append(resample_positive(short_best,n))
    # random smooth
    t=np.linspace(0,1,n,endpoint=False)
    inits.append(normalize_positive(np.maximum(0.2+0.8*np.sin(np.pi*t)**2,0.01),1.0))
    inits.append(normalize_positive(rng.random(n)+0.01,1.0))
    # remove duplicates? not necessary
    return inits
```

`screen_inits` top-level:
```
def screen_inits(inits, max_time, rng, beta, step_initial, max_active, lambda_iters, best_score=None):
    start=time.monotonic()
    if len(inits)==0: return None, np.inf
    per = max(5.0, max_time / len(inits))
    best_a=None; best_s=np.inf
    for a0 in inits:
       if time.monotonic() >= start + max_time - per/2: break
       t_left = start + max_time - time.monotonic()
       alloc = min(per, t_left)
       if alloc < 5: break
       a_opt,s = run_log_mirror(a0, alloc, rng, beta=beta, step_initial=step_initial, max_active=max_active, lambda_iters=lambda_iters)
       if s < best_s:
          best_s=s; best_a=a_opt
    return best_a,best_s
```

```
def normalize_sum(a, S=1.0):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    s = a.sum()
    if s <= 1e-300 or not np.isfinite(s):
        return np.full(len(a), S/len(a))
    a = a * (S / s)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    return a
```
If clipping changes sum, for S=1 clip not active (except if some >1000 impossible). Good.

`central_binomial_seq` vectorized:
```
def central_binomial_seq(n):
    a=np.ones(n)
    if n>1:
       i=np.arange(1,n,dtype=np.float64)
       ratios=(2*i-1)/(2*i)
       a[1:]=np.cumprod(ratios)
    return a
```
For n large, cumprod stable? ratios ~1 -1/(2i); product ~1/√i; no underflow.

```
def prune_population(pop, size):
    # pop list of (score, a)
    # find indices to keep by score ascending
    # get sorted indices without comparing arrays: use list of scores
    scores = [s for s,_ in pop]  # list comprehension no lambda? list comprehension okay (not lambda)
    order = np.argsort(scores)
    return [pop[int(i)] for i in order[:size]]
```
No lambda. But list comprehensions okay. Or use manual: `population.sort(key=None)` not. We'll implement a top-level `prune_population(population, max_size)`.
```
def prune_population(population, max_size):
    if len(population) <= max_size: return population
    scores = np.array([item[0] for item in population], dtype=np.float64)
    idx = np.argsort(scores)[:max_size]
    return [population[int(i)] for i in idx]
```
This uses list comprehension but no lambda. Allowed? It says no lambda functions; list comprehensions are fine. Good.

```
def project_simplex(v, S): ... (copy)
def lse_weights(c,beta): same
def run_fista(a_init, max_time, rng, beta_init=10, beta_rate=0.003, beta_max=5000, S=None):
    n=len(a_init)
    if S is None: S=np.sqrt(2.0*n)
    a=normalize_positive(a_init,S)
    y=a.copy(); t=1; it=0; best=inf; best_a=a; last=0
    fsize=fft_size(n)
    while time...:
       it+=1
       beta=min(beta_max,beta_init*exp(beta_rate*it))
       A=fft(y,fsize); c=...
       score=2*n*c.max()/(S*S)
       if score<best: update
       if it-last>3000:
           # perturb
           noise=0.002*S/np.sqrt(n)
           y=project_simplex(y+rng.normal(0,noise,n), S)
           t=1; last=it; continue
       w=lse_weights(c,beta)
       g=2*real(ifft(fft(w,fsize)*conj(A)))[:n]
       gnorm=norm
       if gnorm<1e-14: break
       step=1.0/(3.0*beta)  # from previous
       # maybe scale by? previous S sqrt(2n), same.
       x=project_simplex(y-step*g,S)
       t_new=0.5*(1+sqrt(1+4*t*t))
       y=x+((t-1)/t_new)*(x-a)
       y=np.maximum(y,0)
       a=x; t=t_new
    return best_a,best/(?) return best score
```

Let's write `run_log_mirror` precisely.

```
def run_log_mirror(a_init, max_time, rng, beta=20.0, step_initial=0.3, max_active=4096, lambda_iters=8, refine_freq=32):
    a = normalize_positive(a_init, 1.0)
    n = len(a)
    if n == 0:
       return a, np.inf
    fsize = fft_size(n)
    size = 2*n - 1
    two_n = 2.0 * float(n)
    max_active = int(max_active)
    max_active = max(2, min(max_active, size))
    start = time.monotonic()
    # initial A,c
    A = fft(a, fsize)
    c = np.real(ifft(A*A, fsize))[:size]
    np.maximum(c, c, out=c)
    best_score = two_n * float(c.max())
    best_a = a.copy()
    prev_score = best_score
    it = 0
    step = float(step_initial)
    max_step = 1.0
    min_step = 0.02
    last_improve = 0
    stale = 2000
    beta_cur = float(beta)
    if max_time > 600:
       refine_freq = max(32, refine_freq)
    lam = choose_lambda(c, a, A, fsize, beta_cur, max_active, lambda_iters)
    if lam is None: lam = lse_weights(c, beta_cur)
    W = fft(lam, fsize)
    while time.monotonic() - start < max_time:
       it += 1
       A = fft(a, fsize)
       c = np.real(ifft(A*A, fsize))[:size]
       np.maximum(c, c, out=c)
       score = two_n * float(c.max())
       if not np.isfinite(score):
           a = best_a.copy()
           continue
       if score < best_score - 1e-14:
           best_score = score
           best_a = a.copy()
           last_improve = it
       # detect bad move
       if score > best_score * 1.03 + 1e-12:
           a = best_a.copy()
           prev_score = best_score
           step = max(min_step, 0.5 * step)
           continue
       # step adapt
       if score < prev_score - 1e-14:
           step = min(max_step, step * 1.02)
       elif score > prev_score + max(1e-10, 1e-8 * prev_score):
           step = max(min_step, step * 0.9)
       prev_score = score
       if it - last_improve > stale:
           noise = 0.05 * np.exp(-0.5 * float(it - last_improve) / float(stale))
           a = normalize_sum(a * np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, noise, n)))
           step = max(min_step, 0.5 * step)
           beta_cur = min(3000.0, beta_cur * 1.2)
           last_improve = it
           continue
       if it % refine_freq == 0:
           lam = choose_lambda(c, a, A, fsize, beta_cur, max_active, lambda_iters)
           if lam is not None and np.isfinite(lam).all():
              W = fft(lam, fsize)
           beta_cur = min(3000.0, beta_cur * 1.02)
       g = 2.0 * np.real(ifft(W * np.conj(A), fsize))[:n]
       g = np.maximum(g, 0.0)
       mu = a * g
       smu = float(mu.sum())
       if smu <= 1e-300:
           a = normalize_sum(a * np.exp(rng.normal(0.0,0.03,n)))
           continue
       mu *= (2.0 / smu)
       d = mu - (2.0 / float(n))
       d -= float(np.mean(d))
       maxd = float(np.max(np.abs(d)))
       if maxd < 1e-12:
           if it - last_improve > 300:
               a = normalize_sum(a * np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.02, n)))
               step = max(min_step, 0.5*step)
           continue
       d = np.clip(d, -5.0, 5.0)
       eff_step = step / (1.0 + 10.0 * maxd)
       if eff_step < min_step: eff_step = min_step
       if eff_step > max_step: eff_step = max_step
       a = normalize_sum(a * np.exp(-eff_step * d))
    return best_a, best_score
```
Note: We used `A` from current a. At `it % refine_freq`, choose_lambda uses c computed before update; good. If `continue` after perturb, we skip gradient; next iter. After `maxd<1e-12`, we continue without updating; this could loop many times without progress if KKT but not improving. It will perturb after 300 not improve? But last_improve may be recent if best improved just before; still could waste. Maybe add small perturb if maxd small and score not best? Use `if it - last_improve > 300: perturb` else `continue`. It may consume iterations but maxd small means near KKT; if not improving, after 300 perturb. OK.

`score > best_score * 1.03 + 1e-12`: If trying to approach best_score from below? If best_score is best, score worse by >3% revert. If best_score initially 1.5 and current 1.56 after bad move, revert. If current just above best due noise (1.001), no revert, step shrinks. Good.

`normalize_sum` after perturb may produce values with sum1. Good.

Potential issue: `np.isfinite(lam).all()` on large array every refine; fine. If it returns False, W unchanged? In code above, if lam invalid, W stays old; okay.

`choose_lambda` may use `np.argpartition` with `size` large; if `max_active` > size/2? It chooses top. Good. But if `max_active` is 2 and active count 1, no argpartition. If active count > max_active.

Now `run_basin_hopping_log`:
```
def run_basin_hopping_log(a_init, max_time, rng, local_time=40.0, beta=20.0, step_initial=0.25, max_active=4096, lambda_iters=6):
    a = normalize_positive(a_init,1.0)
    best_a,best_score=run_log_mirror(a, local_time, rng, beta=beta, step_initial=step_initial, max_active=max_active, lambda_iters=lambda_iters)
    start=time.monotonic()
    remaining = max_time - local_time
    if remaining < local_time:
       return best_a,best_score
    population=[(float(best_score), best_a.copy())]
    n=len(a)
    while time.monotonic() - start < max_time - local_time:
       # if no time for local break
       if time.monotonic() >= start + max_time - local_time: break
       progress = min(1.0, (time.monotonic()-start)/max(1.0,max_time))
       scale = 0.12 * (1.0 + 0.8*(1.0-progress))
       r = float(rng.random())
       if len(population) > 1 and r < 0.25:
          base = population[int(rng.integers(len(population)))][1]
       else:
          base = best_a
       mut = fourier_phase_mutate(base, rng, scale)
       a_opt,s = run_log_mirror(mut, local_time, rng, beta=beta, step_initial=min(step_initial,0.25), max_active=max_active, lambda_iters=lambda_iters)
       if np.isfinite(s) and s < best_score:
          best_score=s; best_a=a_opt.copy()
       population.append((float(s), a_opt.copy()))
       if len(population) > 5:
          population = prune_population(population, 5)
    return best_a, best_score
```
Need if `local_time` > max_time: set local_time=max_time*0.8.

`screen_inits`:
```
def screen_inits(inits, max_time, rng, beta=20.0, step_initial=0.3, max_active=4096, lambda_iters=6):
    start=time.monotonic()
    if not inits or max_time <= 5: return None, np.inf
    per = max(10.0, max_time/len(inits))
    best_a=None; best_s=np.inf
    for a0 in inits:
       if time.monotonic() > start + max_time - 5: break
       alloc = min(per, start + max_time - time.monotonic())
       if alloc < 5: break
       a_opt,s = run_log_mirror(a0, alloc, rng, beta=beta, step_initial=step_initial, max_active=max_active, lambda_iters=lambda_iters)
       if np.isfinite(s) and s < best_s:
          best_s=s; best_a=a_opt
    return best_a,best_s
```

`generate_inits`:
```
def generate_inits(n, rng, given=None, seed_seq=None, max_count=4):
    inits=[]
    if seed_seq is not None and len(seed_seq)>0:
       inits.append(normalize_positive(seed_seq,1.0))
    if given is not None and len(given)>0:
       if len(given)==n:
          g=normalize_positive(given,1.0)
       else:
          g=resample_positive(given,n)
       # avoid duplicate? just append
       if not inits:
          inits.append(g)
       else:
          inits.append(g)
    c=central_binomial_seq(n)
    inits.append(normalize_positive(c,1.0))
    inits.append(normalize_positive(c[::-1].copy(),1.0))
    # optional mixed taper:
    if n>1:
       sym = 0.5*c + 0.5*c[::-1]
       inits.append(normalize_positive(sym,1.0))
    # random smooth
    t=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n,endpoint=False)
    inits.append(normalize_positive(np.maximum(0.1 + 0.9*np.sin(np.pi*t)**2, 0.01),1.0))
    # random log
    inits.append(normalize_positive(np.exp(rng.normal(0,0.5,n)),1.0))
    # limit
    return inits[:max_count]
```

```python
"""
Search for non-negative sequences minimizing
    2*n*max(np.convolve(a,a)) / (sum(a))**2.

The program uses a scale-invariant log-domain multiplicative update:
  * normalize a to sum 1
  * choose a non-negative weight vector on convolution lags
  * compute mu_i = a_i * grad_M_i, scaled so sum(mu)=2
  * update a_i *= exp(-eta*(mu_i - 2/n))
This drives mu toward the KKT fixed point mu_i = constant.

Lag weights are improved periodically by a small convex refinement:
for a set of "active" lags, choose lambda so that mu is as flat as possible.
When the active set is large, an LSE weight vector is used instead, which is
nearly uniform over the active lags.

The search is run at several lengths (8192/16384/32768), with quick screening,
long polishing, and Fourier-domain basin hopping.
"""

import numpy as np
import time
from scipy.fft import fft, ifft


def fft_size(n):
    size = 2 * int(n) - 1
    if size <= 1:
        return 1
    return 1 << (size - 1).bit_length()


def normalize_sum(a, S=1.0):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    a = np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300:
        return np.full(len(a), S / float(len(a)), dtype=np.float64)
    a = a * (float(S) / s)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    return a


def normalize_positive(a, S=1.0):
    a = normalize_sum(a, S)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300:
        return np.full(len(a), S / float(len(a)), dtype=np.float64)
    return a * (float(S) / s)


def central_binomial_seq(n):
    n = int(n)
    if n <= 0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64)
    if n == 1:
        return np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    i = np.arange(1, n, dtype=np.float64)
    ratios = (2.0 * i - 1.0) / (2.0 * i)
    a[1:] = np.cumprod(ratios)
    return a


def resample_positive(src, N):
    src = np.asarray(src, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    if src.size == 0:
        return np.ones(int(N), dtype=np.float64) / float(N)
    src = np.clip(src, 1e-15, 1e15)
    m = src.size
    if m == N:
        return normalize_sum(src, 1.0)
    x_old = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, m)
    x_new = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, int(N))
    y_old = np.log(src)
    y_new = np.interp(x_new, x_old, y_old)
    out = np.exp(y_new)
    return normalize_sum(out, 1.0)


def score_normalized(a):
    n = int(np.size(a))
    if n <= 0:
        return np.inf
    a = normalize_positive(a, 1.0)
    fsize = fft_size(n)
    A = fft(a, fsize)
    c = np.real(ifft(A * A, fsize))[:2 * n - 1]
    np.maximum(c, c, out=c)
    return float(2.0 * n * np.max(c))


def lse_weights(c, beta):
    c = np.asarray(c, dtype=np.float64)
    cmax = float(np.max(c))
    q = float(beta) * (c - cmax)
    # q <= 0, so exp(q) is safe.
    e = np.exp(np.clip(q, -700.0, 0.0))
    s = float(e.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300:
        w = np.zeros_like(c)
        w[int(np.argmax(c))] = 1.0
        return w
    return e / s


def refine_lambda(a, active_idx, A, fsize, max_iters=8, step=0.5):
    n = len(a)
    size = 2 * n - 1
    k = int(np.size(active_idx))
    if k <= 0:
        return None
    if k == 1:
        lam = np.zeros(size, dtype=np.float64)
        lam[int(active_idx[0])] = 1.0
        return lam

    lam = np.zeros(size, dtype=np.float64)
    lam[active_idx] = 1.0 / float(k)

    for _ in range(int(max_iters)):
        W = fft(lam, fsize)
        mu = 2.0 * np.real(ifft(W * np.conj(A), fsize))[:n]
        mu = np.maximum(mu, 0.0)
        smu = float(mu.sum())
        if smu <= 1e-300:
            break
        mu *= (2.0 / smu)

        d = mu - (2.0 / float(n))
        D = fft(d, fsize)
        v = np.real(ifft(D * A, fsize))[:size]

        g = v[active_idx].astype(np.float64, copy=True)
        g = g - float(np.mean(g))
        gmax = float(np.max(np.abs(g)))
        if gmax < 1e-14:
            break

        lr = float(step) / gmax
        la = np.maximum(lam[active_idx], 1e-14).astype(np.float64, copy=True)
        la *= np.exp(np.clip(-lr * g, -10.0, 10.0))
        la /= float(la.sum())
        lam[active_idx] = la

    return lam


def choose_lambda(c, a, A, fsize, beta, max_active, refine_iters):
    size = 2 * len(a) - 1
    cmax = float(np.max(c))
    tol = max(1e-14, 1e-4 * cmax)
    active = np.flatnonzero(c >= (cmax - tol))
    if active.size == 0:
        active = np.array([int(np.argmax(c))], dtype=np.int64)

    max_active = int(max_active)
    if max_active <= 0:
        max_active = 1024
    if max_active >= size:
        max_active = size

    # If the active set is large, use an LSE weight vector, which is almost
    # uniform over the active lags for moderate beta.  This avoids arbitrary
    # tie breaking when top-lag subsetting is used.
    if active.size <= max_active and refine_iters > 0 and active.size >= 2:
        lam = refine_lambda(a, active, A, fsize, max_iters=int(refine_iters), step=0.5)
        if lam is not None:
            if np.all(np.isfinite(lam)):
                lam_sum = float(lam.sum())
                if lam_sum > 1e-300:
                    return lam / lam_sum
    # Fallback: LSE over all lags.  Cap beta so that many nearly-active lags
    # remain nearly uniform.
    b = float(beta)
    if active.size > max_active:
        b = min(b, 100.0)
    return lse_weights(c, b)


def run_log_mirror(a_init, max_time, rng, beta=20.0, step_initial=0.3,
                   max_active=40000, lambda_iters=8, refine_freq=32):
    a = normalize_positive(a_init, 1.0)
    n = int(len(a))
    if n <= 0:
        return a, np.inf

    max_time = float(max_time)
    if max_time <= 0.0:
        return a, score_normalized(a)

    fsize = fft_size(n)
    size = 2 * n - 1
    two_n = 2.0 * float(n)

    # Initial evaluation.
    A = fft(a, fsize)
    c = np.real(ifft(A * A, fsize))[:size]
    np.maximum(c, c, out=c)
    best_score = float(two_n * np.max(c))
    best_a = a.copy()
    prev_score = best_score

    start = time.monotonic()
    it = 0
    step = float(step_initial)
    max_step = 1.0
    min_step = 0.02
    last_improve = 0
    stale = 1800
    beta_cur = float(beta)

    lam = choose_lambda(c, a, A, fsize, beta_cur, max_active, lambda_iters)
    if lam is None or not np.all(np.isfinite(lam)):
        lam = lse_weights(c, beta_cur)
    W = fft(lam, fsize)

    while time.monotonic() - start < max_time:
        it += 1

        A = fft(a, fsize)
        c = np.real(ifft(A * A, fsize))[:size]
        np.maximum(c, c, out=c)
        score = float(two_n * np.max(c))

        if not np.isfinite(score):
            a = best_a.copy()
            continue

        if score < best_score - 1e-14:
            best_score = score
            best_a = a.copy()
            last_improve = it

        # If this move made the current point badly worse than the stored best,
        # revert.  The best point is still kept.
        if score > best_score * 1.03 + 1e-12:
            a = best_a.copy()
            prev_score = best_score
            step = max(min_step, 0.5 * step)
            continue

        # Adaptive simple step control.
        if score < prev_score - 1e-14:
            step = min(max_step, step * 1.02)
        elif score > prev_score + max(1e-10, 1e-8 * prev_score):
            step = max(min_step, step * 0.90)
        prev_score = score

        if it - last_improve > stale:
            noise = 0.05 * np.exp(-0.5 * float(it - last_improve) / float(stale))
            a = normalize_sum(a * np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, noise, n)))
            step = max(min_step, 0.5 * step)
            beta_cur = min(3000.0, beta_cur * 1.2)
            last_improve = it
            continue

        if it % int(refine_freq) == 0:
            lam_new = choose_lambda(c, a, A, fsize, beta_cur, max_active, lambda_iters)
            if lam_new is not None and np.all(np.isfinite(lam_new)):
                W = fft(lam_new, fsize)
            beta_cur = min(3000.0, beta_cur * 1.02)

        g = 2.0 * np.real(ifft(W * np.conj(A), fsize))[:n]
        g = np.maximum(g, 0.0)

        mu = a * g
        smu = float(mu.sum())
        if smu <= 1e-300:
            a = normalize_sum(a * np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.03, n)))
            continue

        mu *= (2.0 / smu)
        d = mu - (2.0 / float(n))
        d -= float(np.mean(d))

        maxd = float(np.max(np.abs(d)))
        if maxd < 1e-12:
            # Flat KKT pressure: do a small perturbation to avoid stalling.
            a = normalize_sum(a * np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.015, n)))
            step = max(min_step, 0.5 * step)
            beta_cur = min(3000.0, beta_cur * 1.05)
            continue

        d = np.clip(d, -5.0, 5.0)
        eff_step = step / (1.0 + 10.0 * maxd)
        if eff_step < min_step:
            eff_step = min_step
        if eff_step > max_step:
            eff_step = max_step

        a = normalize_sum(a * np.exp(-eff_step * d))

    return best_a, best_score


def fourier_phase_mutate(a, rng, scale=0.1):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    n = int(len(a))
    S = float(a.sum())
    if S <= 1e-300:
        S = 1.0
    fsize = fft_size(n)
    A = fft(a, fsize)
    mag = np.abs(A)
    phase = np.angle(A)
    freqs = np.arange(fsize, dtype=np.float64)
    weight = 1.0 + 0.25 * (freqs / float(max(1, fsize)))
    noise = rng.normal(0.0, float(scale) * weight, fsize)
    new_A = mag * np.exp(1j * (phase + noise))
    new_A[0] = A[0]
    out = np.real(ifft(new_A, fsize))[:n]
    out = np.maximum(out, 0.0)
    return normalize_sum(out, S)


def prune_population(population, max_size):
    population = list(population)
    if len(population) <= int(max_size):
        return population
    scores = np.array([float(x[0]) for x in population], dtype=np.float64)
    keep = np.argsort(scores)[:int(max_size)]
    return [population[int(i)] for i in keep]


def run_basin_hopping_log(a_init, max_time, rng, local_time=45.0,
                          beta=20.0, step_initial=0.25,
                          max_active=40000, lambda_iters=6):
    max_time = float(max_time)
    if max_time <= 0.0:
        return normalize_positive(a_init, 1.0), np.inf

    local_time = float(local_time)
    if local_time <= 0.0:
        local_time = max(5.0, max_time * 0.5)
    if local_time >= max_time:
        local_time = max(1.0, max_time * 0.7)

    a = normalize_positive(a_init, 1.0)
    best_a, best_score = run_log_mirror(
        a, local_time, rng,
        beta=beta, step_initial=step_initial,
        max_active=max_active, lambda_iters=lambda_iters
    )

    start = time.monotonic()
    population = [(float(best_score), best_a.copy())]
    min_local = max(10.0, min(60.0, local_time * 0.75))

    while time.monotonic() - start < max_time - min_local:
        if time.monotonic() >= start + max_time - min_local:
            break
        progress = min(1.0, (time.monotonic() - start) / float(max(1.0, max_time)))
        scale = 0.10 * (1.0 + 0.8 * (1.0 - progress))
        if scale < 0.01:
            scale = 0.01

        r = float(rng.random())
        if len(population) > 1 and r < 0.25:
            base = population[int(rng.integers(len(population)))][1]
        else:
            base = best_a

        mut = fourier_phase_mutate(base, rng, scale)
        a_opt, s_opt = run_log_mirror(
            mut, min(local_time, max(10.0, max_time - (time.monotonic() - start) - 5.0)),
            rng,
            beta=beta,
            step_initial=min(0.25, float(step_initial)),
            max_active=max_active,
            lambda_iters=max(3, int(lambda_iters) - 2)
        )

        if np.isfinite(s_opt) and s_opt < best_score:
            best_score = float(s_opt)
            best_a = a_opt.copy()

        population.append((float(s_opt), a_opt.copy()))
        if len(population) > 5:
            population = prune_population(population, 5)

    return best_a, best_score


def generate_inits(n, rng, given=None, seed_seq=None, max_count=4):
    n = int(n)
    inits = []

    if seed_seq is not None and np.size(seed_seq) > 0:
        s = np.asarray(seed_seq, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
        if s.size == n:
            inits.append(normalize_positive(s, 1.0))
        else:
            inits.append(resample_positive(s, n))

    if given is not None and np.size(given) > 0:
        g = np.asarray(given, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
        if g.size == n:
            inits.append(normalize_positive(g, 1.0))
        else:
            inits.append(resample_positive(g, n))

    c = central_binomial_seq(n)
    inits.append(normalize_positive(c, 1.0))
    inits.append(normalize_positive(c[::-1].copy(), 1.0))

    if n > 1:
        sym = 0.5 * c + 0.5 * c[::-1]
        inits.append(normalize_positive(sym, 1.0))

    t = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n, endpoint=False)
    inits.append(normalize_positive(np.maximum(0.1 + 0.9 * np.sin(np.pi * t) ** 2, 0.01), 1.0))
    inits.append(normalize_positive(np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.5, n)), 1.0))

    return [x for x in inits[:max_count] if np.size(x) == n]


def screen_inits(inits, max_time, rng, beta=20.0, step_initial=0.3,
                 max_active=8192, lambda_iters=5):
    inits = list(inits)
    max_time = float(max_time)
    if not inits or max_time <= 5.0:
        return None, np.inf

    start = time.monotonic()
    per = max(8.0, max_time / float(len(inits)))
    best_a = None
    best_score = np.inf

    for a0 in inits:
        now = time.monotonic()
        if now >= start + max_time - 4.0:
            break
        alloc = min(per, start + max_time - now)
        if alloc < 5.0:
            break
        a_opt, s_opt = run_log_mirror(
            a0, alloc, rng,
            beta=beta,
            step_initial=step_initial,
            max_active=max_active,
            lambda_iters=lambda_iters
        )
        if np.isfinite(s_opt) and s_opt < best_score:
            best_score = float(s_opt)
            best_a = a_opt

    return best_a, best_score


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2280):
    rng = np.random.default_rng(int(seed))
    total_start = time.monotonic()
    deadline = total_start + float(budget_s) - 30.0

    best_a = None
    best_score = np.inf
    best_n = None

    # Optional previous construction supplied by the harness.
    given = None
    try:
        graw = height_sequence_1
    except Exception:
        graw = None
    if graw is not None:
        g = np.asarray(graw, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
        g = np.nan_to_num(g, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
        g = np.clip(g, 0.0, 1000.0)
        if g.size >= 16 and np.all(np.isfinite(g)) and np.sum(g) > 0.0:
            given = g

    def update_best(a_candidate, score_candidate):
        nonlocal best_a, best_score, best_n
        if a_candidate is None or not np.isfinite(score_candidate):
            return
        a_candidate = np.asarray(a_candidate, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
        if a_candidate.size == 0:
            return
        if score_candidate < best_score:
            best_score = float(score_candidate)
            best_a = normalize_positive(a_candidate, 1.0).copy()
            best_n = int(best_a.size)

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Phase A: if a good construction is supplied, polish it a little.
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    if given is not None and 2048 <= len(given) <= 32768:
        n0 = int(len(given))
        seed0 = normalize_positive(given, 1.0)
    else:
        n0 = 8192
        seed0 = normalize_positive(central_binomial_seq(n0), 1.0)

    s0 = score_normalized(seed0)
    update_best(seed0, s0)

    remaining = deadline - time.monotonic()
    tA = min(90.0, max(0.0, remaining * 0.04))
    if tA > 30.0:
        aA, sA = run_log_mirror(
            seed0, tA, rng,
            beta=20.0,
            step_initial=0.3,
            max_active=min(40000, 2 * n0),
            lambda_iters=6,
            refine_freq=32
        )
        update_best(aA, sA)

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Phase B: length 16384.
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    remaining = deadline - time.monotonic()
    if remaining > 1700.0:
        tB = 750.0
    elif remaining > 1000.0:
        tB = remaining * 0.55
    else:
        tB = max(0.0, remaining - 20.0)

    if tB > 120.0:
        nB = 16384
        initsB = generate_inits(nB, rng, given=given, seed_seq=best_a, max_count=4)
        screenB = min(120.0, max(20.0, tB * 0.16))
        aB, sB = screen_inits(
            initsB, screenB, rng,
            beta=20.0,
            step_initial=0.3,
            max_active=8192,
            lambda_iters=5
        )
        update_best(aB, sB)

        if aB is None:
            if best_a is not None:
                aB = resample_positive(best_a, nB)
            else:
                aB = normalize_positive(central_binomial_seq(nB), 1.0)

        longB = tB - screenB
        basinB = min(120.0, max(0.0, longB - 380.0))
        longB -= basinB

        if longB > 30.0:
            aB2, sB2 = run_log_mirror(
                aB, longB, rng,
                beta=25.0,
                step_initial=0.3,
                max_active=40000,
                lambda_iters=8,
                refine_freq=32
            )
            update_best(aB2, sB2)
            base_for_basin = aB2
        else:
            base_for_basin = aB

        if basinB > 80.0 and base_for_basin is not None:
            aB3, sB3 = run_basin_hopping_log(
                base_for_basin, basinB, rng,
                local_time=min(45.0, basinB / 8.0),
                beta=35.0,
                step_initial=0.25,
                max_active=40000,
                lambda_iters=5
            )
            update_best(aB3, sB3)

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Phase C: length 32768 if enough time remains.
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    remaining = deadline - time.monotonic()
    tC = max(0.0, remaining - 40.0)
    if tC > 250.0:
        nC = 32768
        initsC = generate_inits(nC, rng, given=given, seed_seq=best_a, max_count=3)
        screenC = min(180.0, max(20.0, tC * 0.15))
        aC, sC = screen_inits(
            initsC, screenC, rng,
            beta=25.0,
            step_initial=0.3,
            max_active=16384,
            lambda_iters=5
        )
        update_best(aC, sC)

        if aC is None:
            if best_a is not None:
                aC = resample_positive(best_a, nC)
            else:
                aC = normalize_positive(central_binomial_seq(nC), 1.0)

        longC = tC - screenC
        basinC = min(180.0, max(0.0, longC - 500.0))
        longC -= basinC

        if longC > 40.0:
            aC2, sC2 = run_log_mirror(
                aC, longC, rng,
                beta=30.0,
                step_initial=0.3,
                max_active=40000,
                lambda_iters=8,
                refine_freq=32
            )
            update_best(aC2, sC2)
            base_for_basin = aC2
        else:
            base_for_basin = aC

        if basinC > 90.0 and base_for_basin is not None:
            aC3, sC3 = run_basin_hopping_log(
                base_for_basin, basinC, rng,
                local_time=min(60.0, basinC / 6.0),
                beta=40.0,
                step_initial=0.25,
                max_active=40000,
                lambda_iters=5
            )
            update_best(aC3, sC3)

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Final short polish of the overall best.
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    remaining = deadline - time.monotonic()
    if best_a is not None and remaining > 60.0:
        tF = min(80.0, remaining - 10.0)
        nF = len(best_a)
        aF, sF = run_log_mirror(
            best_a, tF, rng,
            beta=40.0,
            step_initial=0.25,
            max_active=40000,
            lambda_iters=10,
            refine_freq=16
        )
        update_best(aF, sF)

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Safe return.
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    if best_a is None:
        best_a = normalize_positive(central_binomial_seq(8192), 1.0)

    best_a = np.asarray(best_a, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    best_a = np.nan_to_num(best_a, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    best_a = np.clip(best_a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    best_a = normalize_sum(best_a, 1.0)

    final_list = [float(x) for x in best_a]
    try:
        ver = evaluate_sequence(final_list)
    except Exception:
        ver = np.inf

    if not np.isfinite(ver):
        # Fallback to a simple valid sequence.
        fallback = normalize_positive(central_binomial_seq(8192), 1.0)
        final_list = [float(x) for x in fallback]

    return final_list
```
